C.6
Proof of completeness of Mixed Graded/Linear Logic

We define two categories, one for each fragment of
\mathsf{mGL}{}
,
along with an action on the category corresponding to the graded fragment,
and an adjunction between them.
Then define an equivelence relation on arrows based on the equational theory.
Using the equivelence we define a generic model such that the interpretation of
two derivations are equal exactly when those derivations are equivelent.
Completeness follows straightforwardly from this construction.

Definition 30

(Syntactic categories )
.

Given a preordered semring over
\mathcal{R}
, construct the symmetric monoidal closed category freely from the syntax of graded fragment of
\mathsf{mGL}{}
:

\text{Obj}(\mathbb{G})::=\langle r,\mathsf{J}\rangle\mid\langle r,X\rangle\boxtimes\langle s,Y\rangle\mid\langle r,\mathsf{Lin}\,\mathit{A}\rangle

\text{Hom}_{\mathbb{G}}(\langle r,X\rangle,\langle 1,Y\rangle)=\{t\mid\mathit{r}\odot\mathit{x}:\mathit{X}\vdash_{\mathsf{GS}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{Y}\}

Similarly, construct the symmetric monoidal closed category freely from the syntax of mixed fragment of
\mathsf{mGL}{}
:

\text{Obj}(\mathbb{L})::=\mathsf{I}\mid\mathit{A}\otimes\mathit{B}\mid\mathit{A}\multimap\mathit{B}\mid\mathsf{Grd}_{\mathit{r}}\,\mathit{X}

\text{Hom}_{\mathbb{L}}(A,B)=\{l\mid\emptyset\odot\emptyset;\emptyset\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l}:\mathit{A}\multimap\mathit{B}\}

With
r\in\mathcal{R}
.

Definition 31
.

Let
\odot:\mathcal{R}\times\mathbb{G}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathbb{G}
be

\begin{array}[]{rcl}r\odot\langle s,\mathit{X}\rangle&=&\langle r*s,\mathit{X}\rangle\\
r\odot(\langle s_{1},X\rangle\boxtimes\langle s_{2},Y\rangle)&=&r\odot(\langle s_{1},X\rangle)\boxtimes r\odot(\langle s_{2},Y\rangle)\\
\end{array}

such that for any
r\in\mathcal{R}
and
\langle s,X\rangle\in\text{Obj}(\mathbb{G})
there are natural transformations

\begin{array}[]{rll}0\odot\langle s,X\rangle&\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.44826pt\raise 9.22499pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.36389pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathsf{weak}_{\langle s,X\rangle}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.48654pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.97308pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}&\langle 0,J\rangle\\
(r_{1}+r_{2})\odot\langle s,X\rangle&\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49026pt\raise 6.25278pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.74724pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathsf{contr}_{r_{1},r_{2},X}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.73476pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 55.46951pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}&(r_{1}\odot\langle s,X\rangle)\boxtimes(r_{2}\odot\langle s,X\rangle)\end{array}

Definition 32

(
\mathbb{G}:G\dashv L:\mathbb{L}
)
.

\begin{array}[]{rcl}L:\mathbb{G}\rightarrow\mathbb{L}&&\\
L(\mathsf{I})&=&\langle 1,\mathsf{Lin}\,\mathsf{I}\rangle\\
L(\mathit{A}\otimes\mathit{B})&=&L(A)\boxtimes L(B)\\
L(\mathsf{Grd}_{\mathit{r}}\,\mathit{X})&=&\langle r,X\rangle\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{rcl}G:\mathbb{L}\rightarrow\mathbb{G}&&\\
G(\langle r,\mathsf{J}\rangle)&=&\mathsf{Grd}_{\mathit{r}}\,\mathsf{J}\\
G(\langle r,X\rangle\boxtimes\langle s,Y\rangle)&=&G(\langle r,X\rangle)\otimes G(\langle s,Y\rangle)\\
G(\langle r,\mathsf{Lin}\,\mathit{A}\rangle)&=&\mathsf{Grd}_{\mathit{r}}\,(\mathsf{Lin}\,\mathit{A})\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{lll}\begin{array}[]{lll}&\eta:\langle r,X\rangle\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathsf{L}(\mathsf{G}(r,X))\\
&\eta=\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{G}}\end{array}\\
\\
\begin{array}[]{lll}&\epsilon:\mathsf{Grd}\langle 1,\mathsf{Lin}(A)\rangle\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>A\\
&\epsilon(\langle\mathsf{Grd}_{1}\,(\mathsf{Lin}\,\mathit{A})\rangle)=A\end{array}\end{array}

Lemma 33
.

(\mathbb{G}:G\dashv L:\mathbb{L},\odot)

is an
\mathsf{mGL}{}
model.

Proof.

\odot
is an exponential action and
\mathbb{G}:G\dashv L:\mathbb{L}
is a symmetric monoidal adjunction, so this follows from the definition of an
\mathsf{mGL}{}
: model.
∎

Lemma 34
.

\mathsf{Gr}_{\odot}(\mathcal{R},\mathbb{G})=\mathbb{G}

Proof.

For any
r
,
r\odot\langle 0,J\rangle=\langle 0,J\rangle
So pick
\mathsf{J}
it be
\langle 0,J\rangle

∎

Definition 35

( generic model )
.

Given a preordered semring over
\mathcal{R}
, construct the symmetric monoidal category from the syntax of graded fragment of
\mathsf{mGL}{}
.

\text{Obj}(\mathbb{G}_{\equiv})::=\langle r,\mathsf{J}\rangle\mid\langle r,X\rangle\boxtimes\langle s,Y\rangle\mid\langle r,\mathsf{Lin}\,\mathit{A}\rangle

\text{Hom}_{\mathbb{G}_{\equiv}}(\langle r,X\rangle,\langle 1,Y\rangle)=\{t\mid\mathit{r}\odot\mathit{x}:\mathit{X}\vdash_{\mathsf{GS}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{Y}\}_{/\equiv}

And

\text{Obj}(\mathbb{L}_{\equiv})::=\mathsf{I}\mid\mathit{A}\otimes\mathit{B}\mid\mathit{A}\multimap\mathit{B}\mid\mathsf{Grd}_{\mathit{r}}\,\mathit{X}

\text{Hom}_{\mathbb{L}_{\equiv}}(A,B)=\{l\mid\emptyset\odot\emptyset;\emptyset\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l}:\mathit{A}\multimap\mathit{B}\}_{/\equiv}

With
r\in R
and
\equiv
is the the equivelence relation from the equatonal theory moved across the interpretation.

Note that moving the equivelence relation across the interpretation assumes coherence, that is that derivations of the same judgement are equivelent, to maintain reflexivity. A statement or proof of coherence is omitted, but it follows from the construction of the equational theory.

Lemma 36
.

(\mathbb{G}_{\equiv}:G_{\equiv}\dashv L_{\equiv}:\mathbb{L}_{\equiv},\odot)
is an
\mathsf{mGL}{}
model

Proof.

\odot
is an exponential action and
(\mathbb{G}_{\equiv}:G_{\equiv}\dashv L_{\equiv}:\mathbb{L}_{\equiv},\odot)
is a symmetric monoidal adjunction, so this follows from the definition of an
\mathsf{mGL}{}
: model
∎

See
20

Proof.

If
\llbracket\Pi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rrbracket=\llbracket\Pi_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rrbracket
in all mixed graded/linear models, then

\llbracket\Pi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\rrbracket=\llbracket\Pi_{{\mathrm{2}}}\rrbracket
in

(\mathbb{G}_{\equiv}:G_{\equiv}\dashv L_{\equiv}:\mathbb{L}_{\equiv},\odot)
so

\Pi_{{\mathrm{1}}}\equiv\Pi_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.
∎

C.7
Proof of Substitution for Mixed Graded/Linear Logic

See
21

Proof.

The most interesting cases of this proof are in part i. Thus, we
prove part i in full, and omit the parts ii and iii, because they
are similar. Throughout this proof we use the following definition:

(\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{s_{\mathit{n}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{X},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}:\mathit{X})\in(\delta,\Delta)
iff there exists
\delta^{\prime}
,

\delta^{\prime\prime}
,
\Delta^{\prime}
, and
\Delta^{\prime\prime}
such that
\delta=(\delta^{\prime},\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{s_{\mathit{n}}},\delta^{\prime\prime})
,

\Delta=(\Delta^{\prime},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{X},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}:\mathit{X},\Delta^{\prime\prime})
,
|\delta^{\prime}|=|\Delta^{\prime}|
,
|\delta^{\prime\prime}|=|\Delta^{\prime\prime}|
.

This is a proof by induction on the second assumed typing derivation.

Case.

\frac{\ }{\emptyset\odot\emptyset\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathsf{j}:\mathsf{J}}\quad\textsc{right=$$\text{unit}^{\mathsf{J}}_{I}${}$}

The result holds trivially.

Case.

\frac{\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathsf{J}\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt(\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{s},\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{y}:\mathsf{J},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{Y}}{(\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{s}*\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathsf{let}\,\mathsf{j}\,(\mathit{y})=\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{Y}}\quad\textsc{right=$$\text{unit}^{\mathsf{J}}_{E}${}$}

In this case we have the following assignments:

\begin{array}[]{lll}(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{r_{\mathit{n}}},\delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})=(\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{s}*\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\\
(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{X},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}:\mathit{X},\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})=(\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\\
\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=(\mathsf{let}\,\mathsf{j}\,(\mathit{y})=\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}})\end{array}

It suffices to case split over which vector and context
(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{r_{\mathit{n}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{X},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}:\mathit{X})
fall into. Thus, we have the following cases:

1.

Suppose
(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{r_{\mathit{n}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{X},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}:\mathit{X})\,\in\,(\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}})
. Hence, we have the
following situation:

\frac{\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathsf{J}\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{r_{\mathit{n}}},\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{s},\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{X},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}:\mathit{X},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{y}:\mathsf{J},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{Y}}{(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{r_{\mathit{n}}},\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{s}*\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{X},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}:\mathit{X},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathsf{let}\,\mathsf{j}\,(\mathit{y})=\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{Y}}\quad\textsc{right=$$\text{unit}^{\mathsf{J}}_{E}${}$}

Now by the inductive hypothesis we have

(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\,\cdots\,+\mathit{r_{\mathit{n}}})*\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{s},\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{y}:\mathsf{J},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}[\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\ldots,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}]\mathit{t^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{Y}
.

Next we simply re-apply the rule obtaining:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathsf{J}\quad\quad(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\,\cdots\,+\mathit{r_{\mathit{n}}})*\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{s},\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{y}:\mathsf{J},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}[\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\ldots,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}]\mathit{t^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{Y}}{(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\,\cdots\,+\mathit{r_{\mathit{n}}})*\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{s}*\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\Delta^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathsf{let}\,\mathsf{j}\,(\mathit{y})=\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,[\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\ldots,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}]\mathit{t^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{Y}}\quad\textsc{right=$$\text{unit}^{\mathsf{J}}_{E}${}$}\end{aligned}

We know that
\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}
are all free in
\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
which implies that

\mathsf{let}\,\mathsf{j}\,(\mathit{y})=\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,[\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\ldots,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}]\mathit{t^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}=[\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\ldots,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}](\mathsf{let}\,\mathsf{j}\,(\mathit{y})=\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t^{\prime\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}})
, and thus we
have derived the goal judgment.

2.

Suppose
(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{r_{\mathit{n}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{X},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}:\mathit{X})\,\in\,(\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})
. Similar to the previous case.

3.

Suppose
(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{r_{\mathit{n}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{X},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}:\mathit{X})\,\in\,(\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})
. Similar to the previous case.

Case.

\frac{\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}{(\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\odot(\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}(\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}):\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\boxtimes\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\quad\textsc{right=$$\boxtimes_{I}${}$}

This case follows straightforwardly from the application of the
induction hypothesis to premises of the rule and reapplying the
rule.

Case.

\frac{\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}:\mathit{Y}\hskip 8.19447pt\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}{\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}:\mathit{Y}}\quad\textsc{right=$\textsc{sub}{}$}

with
\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{r_{\mathit{n}}},\delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})

and
\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}=(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{X},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}:\mathit{X},\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})

and
\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}=(\gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{r^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}},\gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}})
.

By induction we have that:

\displaystyle(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\,\cdots\,+\mathit{r_{\mathit{n}}})*\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}[\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\ldots,\mathit{t_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}]\mathit{t^{\prime}}:\mathit{Y}

Since
\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\leq\mathit{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\wedge\ldots\mathit{r_{\mathit{n}}}\leq\mathit{r^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}}
from the structure of
\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\leq\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}

then by reflexivity and monotonicity of
+
and monotonicity of
*
:

(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\,\cdots\,+\mathit{r_{\mathit{n}}})*\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\leq(\mathit{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\,\cdots\,+\mathit{r^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}})*\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}

Thus, we can reapply the sub rule on the inductive hypothesis:

\frac{\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}:\mathit{Y}\hskip 8.19447pt(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},(\mathit{r_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\,\cdots\,+\mathit{r_{\mathit{n}}})*\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\leq(\gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},(\mathit{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\,\cdots\,+\mathit{r^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}})*\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}})}{(\gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},(\mathit{r^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\,\cdots\,+\mathit{r^{\prime}_{\mathit{n}}})*\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}:\mathit{Y}}\quad\textsc{right=$\textsc{sub}{}$}

satisfying the goal.

Case.

\frac{\ }{1\odot\mathit{x}:\mathit{X}\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{X}}\quad\textsc{right=$\textsc{id}{}$}

This case holds by assumption and the fact that
1*\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}=\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}
.

Case.

\frac{\delta^{\prime}\odot\Delta^{\prime};\emptyset\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{l}:\mathit{B}}{\delta^{\prime}\odot\Delta^{\prime}\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathsf{Lin}\,\mathit{l}:\mathsf{Lin}\,\mathit{B}}\quad\textsc{right=$$\mathsf{Lin}_{I}${}$}

This case straightforwardly follows by applying the induction
hypothesis and then reapplying the rule.

Case.

\frac{\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\boxtimes\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt(\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{s},\mathit{s},\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{Z}}{(\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{s}*\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathsf{let}\,(\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}})=\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{Z}}\quad\textsc{right=$$\boxtimes_{E}${}$}

This case is similar to the case for
\text{unit}^{\mathsf{J}}_{E}
above.

Case.

\frac{(\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\odot(\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{Z}}{(\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathsf{0},\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\odot(\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{y}:\mathit{Y},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{Z}}\quad\textsc{right=$\textsc{weak}{}$}

If one of
\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}
are
y
, then this case holds trivially, because the
x
would be
free in
\mathit{t}
, but in the case where
y
is not in
\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}
, then this case
holds by applying the induction hypothesis and reapplying the
rule.

Case.

\frac{(\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\odot(\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{Y},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{Y},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{Z}}{(\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{1}}}}+\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\odot(\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{Y},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}[\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}/\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}]\mathit{t}:\mathit{Z}}\quad\textsc{right=$\textsc{cont}{}$}

This case is similar to the previous case. If
\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and/or
\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
are in
\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}
, then we
apply the induction hypothesis replacing both
\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
and

\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
. If
\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}
nor
\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}
are in
\mathit{x_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\,...\,,\mathit{x_{\mathit{n}}}
, then we simply apply the induction
hypothesis, and reapply the rule.

Case.

\frac{(\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\odot(\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{Z}}{(\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\mathit{s_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\odot(\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Delta^{\prime}_{{\mathrm{2}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{Z}}\quad\textsc{right=$\textsc{ex}{}$}

This case is similar to the previous two cases.

∎

C.8
Proof that MS/GS implies MT/GT

Lemma 37

(
\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}
implies
\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}
)
.

The following hold by mutual induction:

1.

If
\delta\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{GS}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{X}
, then
\delta\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{X}
.

2.

If
\delta\odot\Delta;\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l}:\mathit{A}
, then
\delta\odot\Delta;\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{l}:\mathit{A}
.

Proof.

This is a proof by mutual induction on the assumed typing
derivation. We consider part one first, but only supply the case
for tensor left, because all other cases are either a result of
substitution for typing or are trivial.

Case.

\frac{(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r},\mathit{r},\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GS}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}:\mathit{X}}{(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r},\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\boxtimes\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GS}}\mathsf{let}\,(\mathit{x},\mathit{y})=\mathit{z}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t^{\prime}}:\mathit{X}}\quad\textsc{right=$$\boxtimes_L${}$}

This rule can be derived in
\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}
using the tensor
elimination rule and substitution. First, by the induction
hypothesis we know that:

(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r},\mathit{r},\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}:\mathit{X}

holds. Now we have the following derivation:

\frac{\frac{\,}{1\odot\mathit{z}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\boxtimes\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{z}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\boxtimes\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\quad\textsc{flushleft,right=$\textsc{id}{}$}\quad(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r},\mathit{r},\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}:\mathit{X}}{(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r}*1,\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\boxtimes\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GS}}\mathsf{let}\,(\mathit{x},\mathit{y})=\mathit{z}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t^{\prime}}:\mathit{X}}\quad\textsc{flushleft,right=$$\boxtimes_{E}${}$}

However,
\mathit{r}*1=\mathit{r}
, so let
\delta^{\prime}=(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r}*1,\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}})
, and we obtain our desired derivation.
Finally, we can see that the derived term matches our original
term. Thus, we obtain our result.

We now move onto part two. We consider the following cases:

Case.

\frac{\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}};\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l^{\prime\prime}}:\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}};(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l^{\prime}}:\mathit{A}}{(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}});(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\multimap\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}[\mathit{z}\,\mathit{l^{\prime\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{l^{\prime}}:\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{right=$$\multimap_{L}${}$}

By the induction hypothesis we can see that the following hold:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}};\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{l^{\prime\prime}}:\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\\
\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}};(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{l^{\prime}}:\mathit{A}\end{array}

Next we have the following derivation:

\frac{\frac{\,}{\emptyset\odot\emptyset;\mathit{z}:\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\multimap\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{z}:\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\multimap\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\quad\textsc{flushleft,right=$\textsc{id}{}$}\quad\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}};\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{l^{\prime\prime}}:\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{1}}}}}{\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}};\mathit{z}:\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\multimap\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{z}\,\mathit{l^{\prime\prime}}:\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{2}}}}}\quad\textsc{flushleft,right=$$\multimap_{E}${}$}

Now using substitution for typing (Lemma
21
) with
\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}};(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{l^{\prime}}:\mathit{A}
and
the previous derivation we have

(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}});(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\multimap\mathit{B_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}[\mathit{z}\,\mathit{l^{\prime\prime}}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{l^{\prime}}:\mathit{A}
. So let
\delta^{\prime}=(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}})
.

Case.

\frac{\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}};(\mathit{x}:\mathit{B},\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l^{\prime}}:\mathit{A}}{(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},1)\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:\mathsf{Lin}\,\mathit{B});\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}[\mathsf{Unlin}\,\mathit{z}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{l^{\prime}}:\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{right=$$\mathsf{Lin}_L${}$}

We know the following holds by the induction hypothesis:

\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}};(\mathit{x}:\mathit{B},\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{l^{\prime}}:\mathit{A}

Next we have the following derivation:

\frac{\frac{\,}{1\odot\mathit{z}:\mathsf{Lin}\,\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{z}:\mathsf{Lin}\,\mathit{B}}\quad\textsc{flushleft,right=$\textsc{id}{}$}}{1\odot\mathit{z}:\mathsf{Lin}\,\mathit{B};\emptyset\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathsf{Unlin}\,\mathit{z}:\mathit{B}}\quad\textsc{flushleft,right=$$\mathsf{Lin}_{E}${}$}

Now using substitution for typing (Lemma
21
) with
\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}};(\mathit{x}:\mathit{B},\Gamma)\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l^{\prime}}:\mathit{A}
and
the previous derivation we have:

\delta^{\prime}\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:\mathsf{Lin}\,\mathit{B});\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}[\mathsf{Unlin}\,\mathit{z}/\mathit{x}]\mathit{l^{\prime}}:\mathit{A}

for some
\delta^{\prime}
with
(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},1)\leq\delta^{\prime}
. Thus, we obtain our result.

Case.

\frac{(\delta,\mathit{r})\odot(\Delta,\mathit{x}:\mathit{X});\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l^{\prime}}:\mathit{A}}{\delta\odot\Delta;(\mathit{z}:\mathsf{Grd}_{\mathit{r}}\,\mathit{X},\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathsf{let}\,\mathsf{Grd}\,\mathit{r}\,\mathit{x}=\mathit{z}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{l^{\prime}}:\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{right=$$\mathsf{Grd}_L${}$}

We know the following holds by induction hypothesis:

(\delta,\mathit{r})\odot(\Delta,\mathit{x}:\mathit{X});\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{l^{\prime}}:\mathit{A}

Next we have the following derivation:

\frac{\frac{\,}{\emptyset\odot\emptyset;\mathit{z}:\mathsf{Grd}_{\mathit{r}}\,\mathit{X}\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{z}:\mathsf{Grd}_{\mathit{r}}\,\mathit{X}}\quad\textsc{flushleft,right=$\textsc{id}{}$}\quad(\delta,\mathit{r})\odot(\Delta,\mathit{x}:\mathit{X});\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{l^{\prime}}:\mathit{A}}{\delta\odot\Delta;(\mathit{z}:\mathsf{Grd}_{\mathit{r}}\,\mathit{X},\Gamma^{\prime})\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathsf{let}\,\mathsf{Grd}\,\mathit{r}\,\mathit{x}=\mathit{z}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{l^{\prime}}:\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{flushleft,right=$$\mathsf{Grd}_{E}${}$}

Here choose
\delta^{\prime}=\delta
, and as we can see we obtain our result.

∎

C.9
Proof that MT/GT implies MS/GS

Lemma 38

(
\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}
implies
\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}
)
.

The following hold by mutual induction:

1.

If
\delta\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{X}
, then
\delta\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{GS}}\mathit{t}:\mathit{X}
.

2.

If
\delta\odot\Delta;\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{l}:\mathit{A}
, then
\delta\odot\Delta;\Gamma\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l}:\mathit{A}
.

Proof.

This is a proof by mutual induction on the assumed typing
derivation. We only give the most interesting cases, and the others
are either similar or hold trivially. First, we consider part one.

Case.

\frac{\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\boxtimes\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r},\mathit{r},\delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime\prime}}:\mathit{X}}{(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r}*\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathsf{let}\,(\mathit{x},\mathit{y})=\mathit{t^{\prime}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t^{\prime\prime}}:\mathit{X}}\quad\textsc{right=$$\boxtimes_{E}${}$}

We know the following hold by induction hypothesis:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{GS}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\boxtimes\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\\
(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r},\mathit{r},\delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GS}}\mathit{t^{\prime\prime}}:\mathit{X}\end{array}

Now we have the following derivation:

\frac{\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t^{\prime}}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\boxtimes\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}}\quad\frac{(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r},\mathit{r},\delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}},\mathit{y}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GS}}\mathit{t^{\prime\prime}}:\mathit{X}}{(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r},\delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\boxtimes\mathit{Y_{{\mathrm{2}}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GS}}\mathsf{let}\,(\mathit{x},\mathit{y})=\mathit{z}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t^{\prime\prime}}:\mathit{X}}\quad\textsc{flushleft,right=$$\boxtimes_L${}$}}{(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r}*\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathsf{let}\,(\mathit{x},\mathit{y})=\mathit{t^{\prime}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{t^{\prime\prime}}:\mathit{X}}\quad\textsc{flushleft,right=$\textsc{cut${}_{\mathsf{GS}}$}{}$}

And as we can see we obtain our result.

We now consider part two.

Case.

\frac{\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}};\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}};\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{B}\multimap\mathit{A}}{(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}});(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{right=$$\multimap_{E}${}$}

By the induction hypothesis we know the following hold:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}};\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}\\
\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}};\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{B}\multimap\mathit{A}\end{array}

Now we have the following derivation:

\displaystyle\frac{\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}};\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathit{B}\multimap\mathit{A}\quad\frac{\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}};\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}\hskip 8.19447pt\frac{\,}{\emptyset\odot\emptyset;\mathit{x}:\mathit{B}\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{B}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{flushleft,right=$\textsc{id${}_{\mathsf{MS}}$}{}$}}{\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}};(\mathit{z}:\mathit{A}\multimap\mathit{B},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{z}\,\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{B}}\quad\textsc{flushleft,right=$$\multimap_{L}${}$}}{(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}});(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{flushleft,right=$\textsc{cut${}_{\mathsf{MS}}$}{}$}

And as we can see we obtain our result.

Case.

\frac{\delta\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{GT}}\mathit{t}:\mathsf{Lin}\,\mathit{A}}{\delta\odot\Delta;\emptyset\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathsf{Unlin}\,\mathit{t}:\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{right=$$\mathsf{Lin}_{E}${}$}

By the induction hypothesis we know the following hold:

\delta\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{GS}}\mathit{t}:\mathsf{Lin}\,\mathit{A}

Now we have the following derivation:

\frac{\delta\odot\Delta\vdash_{\mathsf{GS}}\mathit{t}:\mathsf{Lin}\,\mathit{A}\quad\frac{\frac{\,}{\emptyset\odot\emptyset;\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{x}:\mathit{A}}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{flushleft,right=$\textsc{id${}_{\mathsf{MS}}$}{}$}\hskip 8.19447pt}{1\odot\mathit{z}:\mathsf{Lin}\,\mathit{A};\emptyset\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathsf{Unlin}\,\mathit{z}:\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{flushleft,right=$$\mathsf{Lin}_L${}$}}{1*\delta\odot\Delta;\emptyset\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathsf{Unlin}\,\mathit{t}:\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{flushleft,right=$\textsc{gcut${}_{\mathsf{MS}}$}{}$}

And we obtain our result, because
1*\delta=\delta
.

Case.

\frac{\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}};\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathsf{Grd}_{\mathit{r}}\,\mathit{X}\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r},\delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:\mathit{X},\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}});\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A}}{(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}});(\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}})\vdash_{\mathsf{MT}}\mathsf{let}\,\mathsf{Grd}\,\mathit{r}\,\mathit{x}=\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{right=$$\mathsf{Grd}_{E}${}$}

We know by the induction hypothesis that the following hold:

\begin{array}[]{lll}\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}};\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathsf{Grd}_{\mathit{r}}\,\mathit{X}\\
(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r},\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:\mathit{X},\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}});\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A}\end{array}

Now we have the following derivation:

\displaystyle\frac{\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}}\odot\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}};\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{1}}}}:\mathsf{Grd}_{\mathit{r}}\,\mathit{X}\quad\frac{(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{r},\delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{x}:\mathit{X},\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}});\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A}}{(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}});\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\mathit{z}:\mathsf{Grd}_{\mathit{r}}\,\mathit{X}\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathsf{let}\,\mathsf{Grd}\,\mathit{r}\,\mathit{x}=\mathit{z}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{flushleft,right=$$\mathsf{Grd}_L${}$}}{(\delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\delta_{{\mathrm{3}}})\odot(\Delta_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{2}}},\Delta_{{\mathrm{3}}});\Gamma_{{\mathrm{1}}},\Gamma_{{\mathrm{2}}}\vdash_{\mathsf{MS}}\mathsf{let}\,\mathsf{Grd}\,\mathit{r}\,\mathit{x}=\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{1}}}}\,\mathsf{in}\,\mathit{l_{{\mathrm{2}}}}:\mathit{A}}\quad\textsc{flushleft,right=$\textsc{cut${}_{\mathsf{MS}}$}{}$}

In the above we leave the use of exchange implicit. As we can see we have obtained our result.

∎
